The per-class constants\n(:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`,\n:data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`) are kept so callers can branch on\nintent without coupling the failure classes; this constant is the\naggregate ``SuiteLoader``-boundary alias used by CLI tests.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnresolvedCapability(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a required capability cannot be satisfied at load time.\n\n    Surfaced by :class:`SuiteLoader` after :func:`validate_eval_id` has\n    accepted every static id but before parameterize expansion runs. The\n    exception names the capabilities that failed so reporters can render\n    them verbatim; CLI callers MUST map this to\n    :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        eval_id: The eval whose ``requires`` clause could not be\n            satisfied.\n        missing: Tuple of capability names that the injected\n            :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, eval_id: str, missing: Iterable[str]) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.missing: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(missing)\n        missing_list = \", \".join(self.missing) if self.missing else \"<none>\"\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval {eval_id!r} requires unresolved capabilities: {missing_list}\"\n        )\n\n\n# Convenience alias so callers can catch any loader-layer failure with a\n# single ``except SuiteLoaderError`` clause. We intentionally do not make\n# the three concrete classes inherit from a new base — the per-class\n# subclassing already keeps them disjoint, and a base class would force\n# downstream callers (and existing tests) to deal with a new ABC.\nSuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)\n\n\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Pluggable contract the SuiteLoader uses to gate ``requires`` clauses.\n\n    The Protocol intentionally has a single method so the M1 SuiteLoader\n    can be wired before COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates`` (T01.11) lands. The\n    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n    capability names (which the loader turns into\n    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n\n    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n\n        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n        \"\"\"\n        ...\n\n\nclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n\n    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n    no-op default. The real ``CapabilityGates`` implementation lands in\n    T01.11 and is wired through ``commands.py`` (T01.13).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        return ()\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    \"\"\"Schema-validated, capability-checked, parameterize-expanded suite.\n\n    The :class:`SuiteLoader.load` return shape. Every field is a verbatim\n    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n\n    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n       network MCP-reachability probes.\n    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n\n    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n\n    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n        Args:\n            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Suite-level metadata\n            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n            ``defaults``, etc.\n\n        Raises:\n            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n                violation. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`.\n            InvalidEvalId: A static or post-expansion eval id failed\n                :func:`validate_eval_id`. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`. Raised BEFORE any\n                capability resolution call (verified by mocked test).\n            UnresolvedCapability: The injected\n                :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected one or more\n                ``requires`` entries. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        manifest_path = Path(path)\n\n        # Stage 1+2: schema and read. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n\n        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n\n        # Stage 5: parameterize expansion + post-expansion id re-check.\n        # Static ids without ``parameterize`` are passed through; ids\n        # with ``parameterize`` produce one expanded EvalSpec per row.\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            expanded.extend(self._expand_entry(entry))\n\n        return ParsedSuite(\n            name=manifest[\"name\"],\n            version=manifest[\"version\"],\n            description=manifest[\"description\"],\n            defaults=manifest.get(\"defaults\", {}),\n            required_binaries=tuple(manifest.get(\"required_binaries\", ())),\n            optional_capabilities=tuple(manifest.get(\"optional_capabilities\", ())),\n            evals=tuple(expanded),\n            source_path=manifest_path,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _check_capabilities(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Funnel one eval's ``requires`` clause through the resolver.\"\"\"\n\n        required = tuple(entry.get(\"requires\", ()))\n        missing = tuple(self.capability_resolver.resolve(entry[\"id\"], required))\n        if missing:\n            raise UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=entry[\"id\"], missing=missing)\n\n    def _expand_entry(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise ``parameterize`` rows into one ``EvalSpec`` per row.\n\n        Expansion convention (design-spec §5):\n\n        * Base id with no ``parameterize`` → one ``EvalSpec`` with the\n          static id unchanged.\n        * Base id with N ``parameterize`` rows → N ``EvalSpec`` rows\n          whose ids are ``{base}.{1..N}``.\n\n        Each expanded id is re-checked against :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        before it returns — this is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application\n        that the security model relies on. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n        loader's job is to preserve the row data on the expanded\n        ``EvalSpec`` (currently round-tripped via ``EvalSpec.from_dict``\n        keeping ``parameterize`` populated) so the runner can apply\n        substitutions per-id.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        parameterize_rows = entry.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n        if not parameterize_rows:\n            return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry)]\n\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        base_id = entry[\"id\"]\n        for index, _row in enumerate(parameterize_rows, start=1):\n            expanded_id = f\"{base_id}.{index}\"\n            # FR-SCH2 re-check — see method docstring for rationale.\n            validate_eval_id(expanded_id)\n            expanded_entry = dict(entry)\n            expanded_entry[\"id\"] = expanded_id\n            expanded.append(EvalSpec.from_dict(expanded_entry))\n        return expanded\n","numLines":624,"startLine":1,"totalLines":624}}}

None,\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``path`` and verify it lives under an AC12 allowed root.\n\n    The helper is the single ingress point for the AC12 allowlist policy\n    (see roadmap R-016 / FR-ISO2). Callers that mint scratch directories,\n    per-eval HOMEs, or output dirs MUST route the target through this\n    function *before* any filesystem write so a malformed path cannot\n    leak side effects onto disk.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Candidate scratch path. ``str`` accepted for ergonomics;\n            normalised to ``Path``. The helper is the single ingress point for\nthe AC12 scratch-root allowlist; it MUST reject any path that does not\nresolve under ``/tmp/eval-runs``, the repo ``.dev/eval-runs`` directory,\nor a CLI-supplied ``--output-dir``.\n\nCross-links:\n* AC12 / R-016 (this task, T01.19)\n* COMP-005 ``EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`` (T01.01 — single source of\n  truth for the allowlist)\n* FR-ISO2 (T02.08) and NFR-SEC2 (T02.09) — defense-in-depth layered guards\n  inside ``HomeIsolation`` that re-apply the same containment check.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval import (\n    SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE,\n    EvalConfig,\n    ScratchRootViolation,\n    resolve_scratch_root,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval import config as config_module\n\n\n# --- exit-code mapping -----------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_scratch_root_violation_exit_code_is_two() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Aligns with the loader-error trio so operators see a single exit 2.\"\"\"\n\n    assert SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2\n\n\n# --- positive: paths under the canonical allowlist pass --------------------\n\n\ndef test_accepts_path_under_tmp_eval_runs(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"``/tmp/eval-runs/<sub>`` is the canonical M1 scratch root.\"\"\"\n\n    target = Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\") / \"suite-A\" / \"E1\" / \"home\"\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(target)\n    assert resolved == target.resolve()\n\n\ndef test_accepts_tmp_eval_runs_root_itself() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The allowlisted prefix itself must pass (not just strict sub-paths).\"\"\"\n\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(\"/tmp/eval-runs\")\n    assert resolved == Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\").resolve()\n\n\ndef test_accepts_path_under_dev_eval_runs(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Repo-relative ``.dev/eval-runs`` resolves against process CWD.\"\"\"\n\n    repo_relative = Path(\".dev/eval-runs\") / \"suite-A\" / \"E1\"\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(repo_relative)\n    assert resolved == repo_relative.resolve()\n\n\n# --- positive: --output-dir extends the allowlist for the call only -------\n\n\ndef test_accepts_path_under_output_dir(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A CLI-supplied ``--output-dir`` is added to the allowlist for the call.\"\"\"\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"custom-out\"\n    target = output_dir / \"suite-A\" / \"E1\"\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(target, output_dir=output_dir)\n    assert resolved == target.resolve()\n\n\ndef test_output_dir_is_call_scoped_not_persistent(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Passing ``output_dir`` once must not leak into later calls.\"\"\"\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"custom-out\"\n    resolve_scratch_root(output_dir / \"ok\", output_dir=output_dir)\n\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation):\n        resolve_scratch_root(output_dir / \"later\")\n\n\n# --- negative: non-allowlisted prefixes are rejected ----------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"candidate\",\n    [\n        \"/home/user/foo\",\n        \"/var/lib/eval-runs\",\n        \"/etc/passwd\",\n        \"/root/.claude\",\n        \"/usr/local/share\",\n        \"/tmp/other-runs\",  # close-but-not-quite prefix\n    ],\n)\ndef test_rejects_non_allowlisted_paths(candidate: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Every non-allowlisted prefix must raise ``ScratchRootViolation``.\"\"\"\n\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation) as excinfo:\n        resolve_scratch_root(candidate)\n\n    err = excinfo.value\n    assert err.path == Path(candidate)\n    assert err.resolved == Path(candidate).expanduser().resolve(strict=False)\n    # The resolved allowlist used for the check is recorded so doctor /\n    # reporter callers can render it verbatim.\n    assert err.allowed\n    assert Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\").resolve() in err.allowed\n\n\ndef test_violation_message_includes_offending_path() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Forensic detail must survive into the exception message.\"\"\"\n\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation) as excinfo:\n        resolve_scratch_root(\"/home/user/foo\")\n\n    rendered = str(excinfo.value)\n    assert \"/home/user/foo\" in rendered\n    assert \"AC12\" in rendered\n\n\n# --- allowlist sourcing: no module hard-codes a copy -----------------------\n\n\ndef test_allowlist_source_is_evalconfig(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Helper must read the allowlist from the supplied EvalConfig only.\n\n    Mutating ``EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`` (via a fresh instance)\n    must change which paths pass — proving the function does not embed a\n    second hard-coded copy of the allowlist.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    sole_root = Path(\"/tmp/custom-eval-roots\")\n    config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(sole_root,))\n\n    # Default allowlist would have accepted this; the narrowed config rejects.\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation):\n        resolve_scratch_root(\"/tmp/eval-runs/foo\", config=config)\n\n    # And the narrowed allowlist accepts a path under its single entry.\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(sole_root / \"x\", config=config)\n    assert resolved == (sole_root / \"x\").resolve()\n\n\ndef test_default_config_yields_canonical_allowlist() -> None:\n    \"\"\"``EvalConfig()`` defaults are the M1 canonical allowlist (no drift).\"\"\"\n\n    config = EvalConfig()\n    assert Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\") in config.allowed_scratch_roots\n    assert Path(\".dev/eval-runs\") in config.allowed_scratch_roots\n\n\n# --- internals: ergonomics + resolution semantics --------------------------\n\n\ndef test_accepts_str_path_input() -> None:\n    \"\"\"``str`` ergonomics: callers should not have to wrap in ``Path``.\"\"\"\n\n    resolved = resolve_scratch_root(\"/tmp/eval-runs/sub\")\n    assert isinstance(resolved, Path)\n    assert resolved == Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs/sub\").resolve()\n\n\ndef test_relative_path_segments_cannot_escape(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Resolution must collapse ``..`` so traversal cannot bypass the check.\"\"\"\n\n    # Construct a path that textually contains ``/tmp/eval-runs`` but\n    # resolves OUTSIDE the allowlist via ``..`` traversal.\n    escaping = Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs/../etc/passwd\")\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation):\n        resolve_scratch_root(escaping)\n\n\ndef test_uses_expanduser_for_home_anchors() -> None:\n    \"\"\"``~``-prefixed paths must be expanded before allowlist comparison.\"\"\"\n\n    # ``~`` resolves to the real $HOME, which is never on the AC12 list.\n    with pytest.raises(ScratchRootViolation):\n        resolve_scratch_root(\"~/eval-runs/foo\")\n\n\ndef test_resolve_prefix_helper_handles_relative_and_absolute(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"The internal prefix resolver must accept relative + absolute inputs.\"\"\"\n\n    assert config_module._resolve_prefix(Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\")) == Path(\n        \"/tmp/eval-runs\"\n    ).resolve()\n    assert config_module._resolve_prefix(Path(\".dev/eval-runs\")) == Path(\n        \".dev/eval-runs\"\n    ).resolve()\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":252,"cache_read_input_tokens":124225,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":252,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b9529b9d-8809-45ae-abc2-ece65a320f0f","uuid":"f5346c79-e8b1-497b-9dc7-714d4fc4efb8"}

AC12 names the only safe\nlocations: `/tmp/eval-runs/`, repo `.dev/eval-runs/`, or a CLI-supplied\n`--output-dir` resolved against the allowlist; everything else MUST be\nrejected before any filesystem write.\n\n## Public surface\n\n| Symbol | Kind | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| `resolve_scratch_root(path, *, config=None, output_dir=None) -> Path` | function | Resolve `path` and verify it lives under an allowed root. Returns the resolved absolute path on success; raises `ScratchRootViolation` otherwise. |\n| `ScratchRootViolation` | exception | Raised on any non-allowlisted resolution. Carries `path` (original input), `resolved` (absolute form), and `allowed` (resolved allowlist actually used for the check). |\n| `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` | int constant | Exit code (= 2) the CLI maps `ScratchRootViolation` to. Matches the loader-error trio (`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`). |\n\n## Allowlist policy\n\nThe allowlist is `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` and ONLY\n`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. No other module embeds a hard-coded\ncopy — see the `test_allowlist_source_is_evalconfig` assertion in the\ntest set, which narrows the config and proves the helper follows.\n\nDefault contents (set in `_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` per D-0001):\n\n1. `/tmp/eval-runs`\n2. `.dev/eval-runs` (relative; anchors against process CWD via `Path.resolve()`)\n\nThe optional `output_dir` argument extends the allowlist *for the current\ncall only*. It does NOT mutate the supplied config; subsequent calls\nwithout the argument fall back to `config.allowed_scratch_roots` alone.\nThe `test_output_dir_is_call_scoped_not_persistent` assertion locks this\ninvariant.\n\n## Resolution semantics\n\n1. The candidate path is normalised: `Path(path).expanduser().resolve(strict=False)`.\n   `expanduser()` collapses `~`-prefixed home anchors so they cannot land\n   silently inside a sub-tree that happens to contain `eval-runs`.\n   `resolve(strict=False)` allows the directory not to exist yet (M1\n   exit doctor outline runs do not create the scratch tree).\n2. Each allowlist entry is resolved the same way (`_resolve_prefix`).\n   Relative defaults (`.dev/eval-runs`) anchor against the process CWD,\n   matching the behaviour `HomeIsolation` (T02.06) and the CLI `run`\n   command (M2 T02.16) will see at runtime.\n3. The resolved candidate passes iff `resolved == prefix` OR\n   `resolved.is_relative_to(prefix)` for some prefix in the resolved\n   allowlist. Both branches matter: the prefix itself (`/tmp/eval-runs`)\n   is a valid scratch root, not merely a parent of one.\n4. On no match, `ScratchRootViolation` is raised with the original\n   input, the resolved form, and the resolved allowlist embedded so\n   doctor / reporter callers can render the failure verbatim without\n   re-resolving.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `resolve_scratch_root` raises `ScratchRootViolation` for `/home/user/foo`, `/var/lib/eval-runs`, and any non-allowlisted prefix. | Parametrised `test_rejects_non_allowlisted_paths` (6 prefixes covered: `/home/user/foo`, `/var/lib/eval-runs`, `/etc/passwd`, `/root/.claude`, `/usr/local/share`, `/tmp/other-runs`). |\n| Resolved paths under `/tmp/eval-runs/`, `.dev/eval-runs/`, or `--output-dir` pass. | `test_accepts_path_under_tmp_eval_runs`, `test_accepts_path_under_dev_eval_runs`, `test_accepts_path_under_output_dir`. |\n| Allowlist source is `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`; no other module embeds a hard-coded copy. | `test_allowlist_source_is_evalconfig` — narrows the config to a single non-default root and proves the helper follows. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0016/spec.md` documents the allowlist policy. | This file. |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n| Caller | Stage | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `HomeIsolation.setup()` (T02.06 / FR-ISO2) | pre-`mkdtemp` containment check (defense-in-depth) | Re-applies the same helper inside the isolation layer so a loader bypass still fails closed. |\n| `eval run` CLI (M2 T02.16) | `--output-dir` argument resolution | Calls `resolve_scratch_root(output_dir, output_dir=output_dir)` to validate the user-supplied path is itself acceptable (no path traversal, no `~/.claude` etc.). |\n| `eval doctor` (T01.13) | informational | May report the resolved allowlist as part of its `--json` payload so operators can sanity-check the policy before runs. |\n\n## Risk / scope notes\n\n* `Path.resolve(strict=False)` does not exist on Python 3.5; we require\n  Python ≥ 3.10 per `pyproject.toml`, so the API is available.\n* `is_relative_to()` (Python 3.9+) is the comparison primitive; it does\n  NOT perform symlink resolution itself — that is `resolve()`'s job in\n  the same line. Both are needed; either alone is unsafe.\n* Symlink resolution is not separately delegated to `HomeIsolation` here.\n  The isolation layer (T02.07, NFR-SEC2) re-resolves symlinks AFTER\n  scratch creation but BEFORE hook deploy; that second pass is the\n  load-bearing defense against `scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME` attacks.\n  This helper's job is the prefix-allowlist check; the two layers are\n  intentionally distinct.\n\n## Cross-references\n\n* `src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py` — implementation.\n* `tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` — 19 assertions\n  covering exit code, positive prefixes (`/tmp/eval-runs`, `.dev/eval-runs`,\n  `--output-dir`), 6 negative prefixes, allowlist-source-of-truth,\n  ergonomics, and traversal collapsing.\n* `artifacts/D-0001/spec.md` — `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` field\n  schema (the single source of truth this helper consumes).\n* Roadmap entries: AC12 (R-016 / row 16) and OPS-002 (row 43).\n* Design-spec §5 path containment guard (lines 404–409) — the broader\n  policy this helper enforces at the loader / config layer.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":946,"cache_read_input_tokens":130050,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":946,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b9529b9d-8809-45ae-abc2-ece65a320f0f","uuid":"c962207e-cd72-430c-a7dd-c5eef32823a8"}

AC12 names the only safe\nlocations: `/tmp/eval-runs/`, repo `.dev/eval-runs/`, or a CLI-supplied\n`--output-dir` resolved against the allowlist; everything else MUST be\nrejected before any filesystem write.\n\n## Public surface\n\n| Symbol | Kind | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| `resolve_scratch_root(path, *, config=None, output_dir=None) -> Path` | function | Resolve `path` and verify it lives under an allowed root. Returns the resolved absolute path on success; raises `ScratchRootViolation` otherwise. |\n| `ScratchRootViolation` | exception | Raised on any non-allowlisted resolution. Carries `path` (original input), `resolved` (absolute form), and `allowed` (resolved allowlist actually used for the check). |\n| `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` | int constant | Exit code (= 2) the CLI maps `ScratchRootViolation` to. Matches the loader-error trio (`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`). |\n\n## Allowlist policy\n\nThe allowlist is `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` and ONLY\n`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. No other module embeds a hard-coded\ncopy — see the `test_allowlist_source_is_evalconfig` assertion in the\ntest set, which narrows the config and proves the helper follows.\n\nDefault contents (set in `_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` per D-0001):\n\n1. `/tmp/eval-runs`\n2. `.dev/eval-runs` (relative; anchors against process CWD via `Path.resolve()`)\n\nThe optional `output_dir` argument extends the allowlist *for the current\ncall only*. It does NOT mutate the supplied config; subsequent calls\nwithout the argument fall back to `config.allowed_scratch_roots` alone.\nThe `test_output_dir_is_call_scoped_not_persistent` assertion locks this\ninvariant.\n\n## Resolution semantics\n\n1. The candidate path is normalised: `Path(path).expanduser().resolve(strict=False)`.\n   `expanduser()` collapses `~`-prefixed home anchors so they cannot land\n   silently inside a sub-tree that happens to contain `eval-runs`.\n   `resolve(strict=False)` allows the directory not to exist yet (M1\n   exit doctor outline runs do not create the scratch tree).\n2. Each allowlist entry is resolved the same way (`_resolve_prefix`).\n   Relative defaults (`.dev/eval-runs`) anchor against the process CWD,\n   matching the behaviour `HomeIsolation` (T02.06) and the CLI `run`\n   command (M2 T02.16) will see at runtime.\n3. The resolved candidate passes iff `resolved == prefix` OR\n   `resolved.is_relative_to(prefix)` for some prefix in the resolved\n   allowlist. Both branches matter: the prefix itself (`/tmp/eval-runs`)\n   is a valid scratch root, not merely a parent of one.\n4. On no match, `ScratchRootViolation` is raised with the original\n   input, the resolved form, and the resolved allowlist embedded so\n   doctor / reporter callers can render the failure verbatim without\n   re-resolving.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `resolve_scratch_root` raises `ScratchRootViolation` for `/home/user/foo`, `/var/lib/eval-runs`, and any non-allowlisted prefix. | Parametrised `test_rejects_non_allowlisted_paths` (6 prefixes covered: `/home/user/foo`, `/var/lib/eval-runs`, `/etc/passwd`, `/root/.claude`, `/usr/local/share`, `/tmp/other-runs`). |\n| Resolved paths under `/tmp/eval-runs/`, `.dev/eval-runs/`, or `--output-dir` pass. | `test_accepts_path_under_tmp_eval_runs`, `test_accepts_path_under_dev_eval_runs`, `test_accepts_path_under_output_dir`. |\n| Allowlist source is `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`; no other module embeds a hard-coded copy. | `test_allowlist_source_is_evalconfig` — narrows the config to a single non-default root and proves the helper follows. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0016/spec.md` documents the allowlist policy. | This file. |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n| Caller | Stage | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `HomeIsolation.setup()` (T02.06 / FR-ISO2) | pre-`mkdtemp` containment check (defense-in-depth) | Re-applies the same helper inside the isolation layer so a loader bypass still fails closed. |\n| `eval run` CLI (M2 T02.16) | `--output-dir` argument resolution | Calls `resolve_scratch_root(output_dir, output_dir=output_dir)` to validate the user-supplied path is itself acceptable (no path traversal, no `~/.claude` etc.). |\n| `eval doctor` (T01.13) | informational | May report the resolved allowlist as part of its `--json` payload so operators can sanity-check the policy before runs. |\n\n## Risk / scope notes\n\n* `Path.resolve(strict=False)` does not exist on Python 3.5; we require\n  Python ≥ 3.10 per `pyproject.toml`, so the API is available.\n* `is_relative_to()` (Python 3.9+) is the comparison primitive; it does\n  NOT perform symlink resolution itself — that is `resolve()`'s job in\n  the same line. Co-locating the policy check next to the policy data\n   keeps the \"only one source of truth\" guarantee structural rather\n   than aspirational. (Loader-layer typed errors stay in `loader.py`;\n   this is a config-layer policy, not a manifest-validation gate.)\n\n2. **`output_dir` is a kwarg, not a stored config field.** AC12 names\n   `--output-dir` as a *per-invocation* override, not a permanent\n   widening of the allowlist. Keeping it call-scoped means a doctor\n   run cannot accidentally relax future calls inside the same process.\n   The test `test_output_dir_is_call_scoped_not_persistent` locks this\n   in.\n\n3. **Exit code mirrors the loader trio (= 2).** Operators already learn\n   \"exit 2 means the harness refused to start\"; adding a fourth distinct\n   code would dilute that signal. Keeping the constant named separately\n   (`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`) lets call sites still branch on\n   *which* gate fired when logging.\n\n4. **`resolve(strict=False)` over `strict=True`.** M1 outline runs and\n   the eval doctor command may evaluate the policy before any scratch\n   tree exists. `strict=True` would refuse to resolve a not-yet-created\n   directory and force callers to pre-create scratch paths — exactly\n   the side effect AC12 forbids.\n\n5. **Equality branch alongside `is_relative_to`.** `Path.is_relative_to`\n   returns False when both paths are equal in older Python builds (the\n   3.12 stdlib returns True, but staying explicit prevents future\n   regressions and documents intent).\n\n## Open follow-ups\n\n* **FR-ISO2 reuse.** `HomeIsolation.setup()` (T02.06) should call\n  `resolve_scratch_root` rather than re-implementing the check. That\n  wiring lands in Phase 2 — out of scope for T01.19.\n* **`eval doctor --json` allowlist surfacing.** The doctor JSON payload\n  could include the resolved allowlist for operator sanity. T01.13\n  already shipped without it; adding it is non-blocking and tracked\n  loosely under OPS-002 (R-policy enforcement) rather than this task.\n\n## What was deliberately left out\n\n* No symlink-following audit in this helper. Symlink attacks are\n  caught by `HomeIsolation` AFTER scratch creation (NFR-SEC2 /\n  TEST-003), which is the right time to follow them. Doing it here\n  would force `resolve_scratch_root` to walk a not-yet-existing tree.\n* No `--output-dir` mutation of the stored `EvalConfig`. AC12 calls\n  the override per-invocation; folding it into the config would\n  silently widen the allowlist for the rest of the run.\n* No mention of `~/.claude/` as an explicit reject prefix.
